Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

4 COMMENTS
Scott Galuska4 November, 2019 at 15:50
I see that Absolute has both ‘hobby’ and ‘retail’ versions of the base set (cards 1 – 200) listed in the Beckett online checklist. What is the difference between the two?
Ryan Cracknell4 November, 2019 at 16:14
@Scott – It looks like hobby use all foil in the background for the card stock whereas retail does not.
Scott Galuska3 February, 2020 at 14:59
Thanks Ryan! That brings me to my next question- what is the difference between the “all foil” background hobby cards and the spectrum parallels? Seem to be similar, unless I’m missing something…
Ryan Cracknell4 February, 2020 at 02:47
@Scott – I don’t have any but from the looks of card online, Spectrum have serial numbers and are lighter and/or colorful around the borders depending on which ones.

4 COMMENTS
Scott Galuska4 November, 2019 at 15:50
I see that Absolute has both ‘hobby’ and ‘retail’ versions of the base set (cards 1 – 200) listed in the Beckett online checklist. What is the difference between the two?
Ryan Cracknell4 November, 2019 at 16:14
@Scott – It looks like hobby use all foil in the background for the card stock whereas retail does not.
Scott Galuska3 February, 2020 at 14:59
Thanks Ryan! That brings me to my next question- what is the difference between the “all foil” background hobby cards and the spectrum parallels? Seem to be similar, unless I’m missing something…
Ryan Cracknell4 February, 2020 at 02:47
@Scott – I don’t have any but from the looks of card online, Spectrum have serial numbers and are lighter and/or colorful around the borders depending on which ones.

4 COMMENTS
Scott Galuska4 November, 2019 at 15:50
I see that Absolute has both ‘hobby’ and ‘retail’ versions of the base set (cards 1 – 200) listed in the Beckett online checklist. What is the difference between the two?
Ryan Cracknell4 November, 2019 at 16:14
@Scott – It looks like hobby use all foil in the background for the card stock whereas retail does not.
Scott Galuska3 February, 2020 at 14:59
Thanks Ryan! That brings me to my next question- what is the difference between the “all foil” background hobby cards and the spectrum parallels? Seem to be similar, unless I’m missing something…
Ryan Cracknell4 February, 2020 at 02:47
@Scott – I don’t have any but from the looks of card online, Spectrum have serial numbers and are lighter and/or colorful around the borders depending on which ones.

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).

Ryan Cracknell
A collector for much of his life, Ryan focuses primarily on building sets, Montreal Expos and other interesting cards. He's also got one of the most comprehensive collections of John Jaha cards in existence (not that there are a lot of them).
